For 334. Boneworks studied from its files only (C:/Program Files (x86)/Steam/steamapps/common/BONEWORKS, read-only, never
launched; nothing written there). It is IL2CPP (GameAssembly.dll + global-metadata.dat v24.1, Unity 2018.4.10f1, type
trees stripped), so there is no C# to read: a self-made parser of global-metadata.dat (scratchpad bw_meta.py: 11274
types, 70454 methods, 56432 fields) gave every class's fields and methods by name, and its asset files were read with
UnityPy, each script's raw bytes laid out by the field order the parser gave (built-in types and PPtrs decode exactly).

How Boneworks reloads, by its own names:
- Magazine into the gun: a Socket (MagazineSocket, on the well) and a Plug (MagazinePlug : AlignPlug : Plug, on the
  magazine). States EMPTY, HOVERING, SLIDING, INSERTED, EJECTING, SWITCHING. AlignPlug turns the plug onto the socket
  (_isEnterTransition), then slides it on its line (_isStageTwoAnimation, _perc), locks at the end (Socket.Lock,
  OnPlugInsertComplete; MagazineSocket.CoWaitLockMagazine) and backs out past cutoffDirection.
- Measured on 40 guns: plug effectiveRadius 4 cm on every magazine (our gate takes a magazine within 3.5-4.5 cm of the
  well: the same); socket influenceRadius 25 cm everywhere (hover only); endDistance (the insert travel) 5.1-12.7 cm per
  gun, as our rail lengths.
- Slide: the hand holds a SlideGrip (virtualControllerMode SLIDE, grip radius 1.5 cm, minBreakForce = maxBreakForce =
  infinity: a slide grip never breaks by force, only by distance), slideDistance 4 cm (30 pistol slides) or 8 cm (10),
  slideDirection (0,0,1), upDirection (0,1,0), isFlippable on 11 of the 30 pistols (the pose flips with the side the hand
  comes from). The hand's travel along the slide scrubs the gun's slide animation (Gun.UpdateSlidePercentage,
  SlideOverride); let go, it returns on returnCurve. Gun.SlideStates RETURNING/RETURNED/PULLING/PULLED/LOCKED,
  HammerStates, CartridgeStates; CompleteSlidePull ejects and cocks, CompleteSlideReturn chambers (PullCartridge).
- Gun.isSlideReleaseOnCollision (a slammed magazine or knock drops a locked slide) is on for 8 of the 40 guns (the SMGs:
  800-900 rpm, 400 m/s).
- Gun.CheckGunRequirements / RequiresChargingHandle / CoBlinkHighlight(magazineHelperRenderer,
  chargingHandleHelperRenderer): the gun blinks a helper on what it needs next (a magazine, the charging handle).
- Hands are physics bodies: PhysHand is a rigidbody pushed toward the tracked hand by clamped muscle forces (measured:
  sideways 90 N each way, up 90 N, down 200 N, push 340 N, pull 360 N, maxTorque 30, forceMultiplier 1.25, dampening 0.2,
  newtonDamp 80, a strength curve over -1.05..1.05 from 0.2x through 1x to 8x), CheckStuck/SetHand put a stuck hand back on
  the tracked one. So a hand stops at a gun, and a gun heavier than the wrist's 30 N*m sags.
- Grip.Snatch / CoMoveHandToTarget (hand and object glide together on a grab), HandToGripState.isFlipped / poseRadius,
  VirtualController (two-hand pose; RifleShoulder; AimEnhancer turns the gun a little toward the eye-sight line;
  CheckHandDesync + desyncTime break a grip the hand has drifted from).
- Haptics: Haptor presets with a stack policy (Add, Override, Ignore, Duck) and noDoubleHaptic.

Done (2026-10-23, all offline-tested; the suite has checks for the first and the last):
- Glove contact (c0d3fd9): the glove is swept from where it was drawn (in the gun's space) toward the tracked hand,
  stopped at the last clear step and slid along the gun's axes (PhysHand), let go to the real hand once that is clear 15
  cm past it (CheckStuck/SetHand). Fixes the user's two cases: a fast swipe through a thin receiver landed clear on the far
  side (the old search started from the hand's new place), and after a double-magazine reload the promoted magazine's
  hand, still up at the well, had no glove keep-out and slid into the gun. src/Rig/GloveSweep.cs (pure, 8 checks).
- Hands stay on a worked part (97e0c54): a racked bolt, handle, slide or pump, a held feed cover or optic keeps the glove
  on it for as far as the hand holds it (46 cm: the rack's drift 35 cm and the new overshoot 30 cm past the stroke's ends),
  the arm with it; before, 5 cm, so a hand shoved into the gun while racking drew the glove up to 30 cm inside.
- Slam release (c97364a): a magazine seated at 0.9 m/s or more into a locked gun sends the slide forward (config
  MagSlapReleasesSlide, on for every gun; Boneworks has it per gun). Each seat into a locked gun logs its speed.
- Haptic stack (5ffee9a): a weaker pulse does not cut a stronger one still playing (SteamVR replaces the running one, and
  the 45 ms Touch rack ticks cut the slide's click).

Check in the headset: swipe the free hand fast through a pistol and a rifle (it stops at the gun and slides along it),
push it deep in and pull out; rack a charging handle while pushing the hand into the gun (the glove stays on the handle);
a double-magazine reload (the hand never inside the gun); slam and gently push a magazine into a locked gun (the log's
"magazine seated at X m/s" lines give the threshold); the click of the slide and the seat not cut short on Touch.

Not done (candidates): the helper blink (needs a highlight the game's shaders can draw: no custom shaders on this PC),
the HOVERING cue before the rail takes a magazine, a short settle before the seat locks (CoWaitLockMagazine), weapon sag
from the wrist's torque (PhysHand maxTorque 30 against the gun's weight torque) in place of the per-class droop,
AimEnhancer.

### For 336: estimated frame-rate gain (the user asked for it, 2026-10-24; estimates, not measurements)
Worked out from what each preset draws per eye in multi-pass VR (pixels, MSAA samples, shadow passes and the depth pass shadows need, draw distance and LOD), anchored on the one measurement from the user's PC: old Ultra ran 31-46 fps in play. The wrist line ("90 Hz · N% on time") and the log's once-a-minute line measure the real numbers.

| Preset | Old -> new | Est. frame time | Est. fps (was -> now, before VR's rate cap) |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Low | hard shadows 35 m, LOD 0.6, 1 light -> no shadows, LOD 0.4, no pixel lights, 8-bit colour | -30 to -40% | about at the game's own Fastest, which already held the headset's rate |
| Medium | scale 1.0, soft shadows 60 m x2 cascades, LOD 1.0, 2 lights -> scale 0.9, hard 30 m x1, LOD 0.7, 1 light, 8-bit colour | -30 to -40% | |
| High | scale 1.2, MSAA 4x, shadows 100 m high-res, LOD 1.5, 4 lights, reflections -> scale 1.0, MSAA 2x, 60 m medium, LOD 1.0, 2 lights, no reflections | -30 to -45% | ~40-60 -> ~60-90 |
| Ultra | scale 1.4, 4 cascades to 150 m, LOD 2.0, 6 lights -> scale 1.2, 2 cascades to 100 m, LOD 1.5, 3 lights | -25 to -35% | 31-46 (measured) -> ~42-65 |

Smaller ones: the player's hidden body takes its shadow casters (body, gear, the gun's third-person copy) out of every shadow pass, about 0.1-0.3 ms a frame with shadows on; after a hitch the next frame runs at most 6 physics steps instead of up to 20 (one stall instead of two); the allocation fixes save well under 0.1 ms but mean fewer garbage-collection slices (up to 3 ms each).

### 366. Improve and bug hunt to 50% (user, 2026-11-07: "yes please build the setup / after that please continue to /work-until-limit 50%")

The game not started. 1.16.10 built first (setup in `dist/`). `docs/HANDOVER.md` holds where things stand, kept current through the run. Mid-run the user: "Night vision doesnt work at all it just does nothing and isnt even in my vr view / put it on your list / Also while you work document more and make sure that you understand it if things get compacted later...". So this entry is kept as the run goes: what was found, why, and what is left.

- [x] **Night vision in the eyes (the user's report).** Cause, read in the game's files with UnityPy (`resources.assets`, type trees stripped, parsed by hand) and in `PostProcessingManager`: the game has two post-process layers. Each world camera gets one at run time with `volumeLayer` = layer 29 (the AO volume alone, `RegisterWorldCamera`). The look is on a separate last camera, `_Managers/Global Post-processing`, `finalLayer`, `volumeLayer` 0x10000000 = layer 28: `Ingame` (grade, bloom, priority -1) and `_Managers/Default Night Vision Goggles/Night Vision Post Processing` (global, priority 100, inactive until switched; profile `Night Vision` = ColorGrading, Bloom, Grain). `VRRig.UpdatePostProcessing` copied the mask of the world camera, so the eyes had a working post-process layer that could never see those volumes. The goggles' `activatedObject` was read too: it is that volume's object (PPtr at byte 636 of the `NightVision`), so the switch reaches it. Fix: the mask, resources and AA come from `PostProcessingManager.instance.finalLayer`. Task 252 (w)'s claim that the goggles draw in the headset was never seen in one. PostProcessing = true had the same fault: it gave AO and no grade; it now gives both. Log lines for the next play: "Goggles: the hand at the side of the head switched them." and "Night vision on: the eyes' post-processing sees layers 10000000, HDR ...". If the first comes and the view stays plain, the post-processing stack is the next suspect, not the gesture; if the first never comes, the gesture is. Not seen in a headset.

- [x] Full reload on a small action: a mod's reload as ONE clip (MG3 `mg3_r1` 7.1 s, m4a1squad, g17) was bound whole to the nearest action. Clips of 1 s and over are sliced at the actions now, a bank of them too, and nothing plays more than 0.6 s of one (08a1423).

PKM, MK46 and MG3 checked on their bundles: all hinge on the real cover.
- [x] A short cover lifts from anywhere on it (`CoverLever`).
- [x] A look under the cover left a loaded gun dead (LAY BELT with the belt never gone): shut again on the same box it feeds; a box changed under it still has to be laid (reports 274, 312 hold; scripted in AmmoFuzz).
- [x] An empty box under an open cover: the counter says BOX EMPTY and offers no belt to take.
- [x] Belts not laid by hand: a box put in under the open cover shows its belt.
- [x] A magazine in the hand survived death into the next life, letting rounds out on the trigger: a dead hand lets go.
- [x] AS Val (squad, all four) racked by nothing: its bolt moves 8 cm in the fire clip, but the clips are chosen by the game's `random` (0-100 each frame) and the sampler left it at 0, so "random > 0" never played. Set to 1, under every threshold in the 96 bundles (scanned): guns that sampled the first clip at 0 keep it. Also lets the M4 Suzuya's gated reload play. MG3, M4A1, XM8 and MK18 decoded: their fire clips only move the gun body, so they rack as before.
- [x] A refill from outside with chamber plus one off read 8+1 on an 8-round gun.
- [x] The trigger held to leave a seat fired the gun as it came up on foot: let go first now.
- [x] An enemy's gun whose magazine was pulled came back with one seated, reading 0: it comes back with none in, wrested or from his body.
- [x] QJB201 family (9 guns): the mod's muzzle transform sits in the receiver, 57 cm behind the barrel's end, so a 1 m gun was 15 cm long to the mod. Taken at the barrel's end, as a muzzle too far ahead already was. The game still fires from its own.
- [x] MK46 and QJB201: a fresh box's belt was already on the tray beside the one in the hand, and lifting the cover left the old one there. Their rounds are moved out of sight (MK46) or scaled away (QJB201) by the `loaded ammo` clips, no part to hide, and the animator was told the box's count whether the belt was laid or not. It is told 0 until the belt is on the tray.
- [x] The same two guns drew the mod's strip on the tray over their own belt once it was laid: no part named a belt, so the strip was laid "for a gun without a belt in its model". The sampler now sees what goes at 0 rounds (`AmmoDrawsParts`), and such a gun gets no strip. Likely the belt "through the box when turned" of 2026-11-04 on these guns.
- [x] A double barrel dropped and picked up lost its fired cases and came back shut: both kept. The cover weighs less against the belt only while a belt really is next. Stowed gear turns to the hand the gun is in now, not the set one.
- [x] Small ones: a round put back at the pouch after its rifle was dropped goes to that drop, as a magazine's rounds do; no round out of a magazine in the well.
- Scouted clean: loose magazines (no round made from nothing; a full reserve caps what comes back, on purpose), fire modes, the left hand, seats but for the trigger above.
- 1.16.9 built and installed at the user's word ("yes please"). None of it seen in the headset yet: first the QJB201's cover and belt, the MK46's belt, the AS Val's bolt.
- Tool: `tools/bundles/clip_motion.py` decodes what a mod's clip really turns and moves, and a part's real geometry. The QJB201's cover, the AS Val's bolt and the MK46's belt were found with it: the analyser's own reports only show what it saw. Worth running first when a gun misbehaves.
- Open, a design call: the QJB201 [MG&AR]'s T key (the fire-mode press sends it) swaps belt and magazine in its script; the rig stays the belt gun's, so AR mode still reloads as a belt gun.

### 358. Handling details (user, 2026-11-03: "more realistic and detailed ... simple tiny things"; picks 1-3, 5-11; the game not started)

Rule from the user: "Youre not allowed to make sounds on your own. Use game sounds": GameSounds plays slices of the weapon's own
cues (MagIn, MagOut, SlideBack, SlideForward) or a vanilla donor's through the pool (ProceduralSounds.PlayGameClip), never the
synthesised fallback. Built (a6d0f14, 6d198e3, ef1fa0d, 4684427), each to check in a game run:
- 1 Scrape: MagazineProp.ContactSounds, at the well's mouth only (4.5 cm), MagIn's first 30% quiet and lower.
- 2 Rack spring: tug and tick strength 0.35 -> 1 with the stroke (not for hand-driven bolts/pumps); a knock at the back stop.
- 3 Return: StepParts accelerates the slide (250 m/s^2, top 8), the landing jolt by speed; the release's slam volume by how far it
  has to go; a hand-closed bolt/pump by its push speed.
- 5 Clatter: MagazineClatter on magazine props (MagIn's last 40%, lower; softer on CaseSound.IsSoft ground).
- 6 Catch: ReloadInteractions.StepParts on SlideLocked false -> true: SlideBack's end at 1.12 pitch and a tap (no sound right after
  a shot: OnShot plays the gun's own).
- 7 Hand loading: rounds click in (MagIn's end) higher as the magazine fills; a round out (MagOut's start); the pouch's round
  felt only.
- 8 Detent (MagazineDetent on): DetentApplies (not WellInGrip, MG, en-bloc, boxes, autotest), latch 8% of the rail (4-8 mm),
  firm = push 0.3 m/s or 1 cm past the seat; let go there: LetGoOnRail and _almostSeated (tap 0.45 m/s on the base, a squeeze takes
  it, the release drops it); UnderSeatedMagazineFalls (off): AmmoState.MagLoose, ejected at the next shot, tap latches.
- 9 Bolt lift: ReloadInteractions.BoltLifts (Manual, hand-driven, not a pump, a bolt/handle part, rack point 2-12 cm out to the
  side), 60 degrees about the bore before the pull; HeldWeapon.ApplyBoltTurn turns the bolt and handle; down at home by hand or
  when let go.
- 10 AkMagazinesStayIn (off): WeaponRigData.MagCurved rifles, the release only unlatches (MagOut's start, a tap).
- 11 Rock-in: MagazineProp.RailPose turns a curved magazine 14 degrees about its front lip at the mouth, straight by 85% of the
  rail.

### 352. Hands in slow motion: Normal or Slowed (user, 2026-10-28 evening; built 7b4824a, check in a game run)
- [ ] "lets do a mode in settings, Normal and slowed (regarding hands, hands holding mags, hands holding guns, guns etc almost
      everything when its on slowed the hands etc are delayed too in slowmo (everything) like boneworks does it". Built: the
      setting SlowMotionHands (panel: "Hands in slow motion"). Slowed: in slow motion each hand follows its controller in game
      time (VRHand.Slowed: a critically damped spring of 20/s, 10 m/s and 30 rad/s at most, all in game time), so everything
      that follows the hand (glove, held gun and magazine, arms, the gun's stand-in, the fist, a held soldier) is slowed with it;
      the head is not. Its velocities are in game units, so RealTimeFlight and the slow-motion factors are off and what it lets go
      of moves at the world's pace. No technical write-up of Boneworks' slow motion was found online; built from how it plays
      (its physical body runs in game time, the camera does not). Autotest: SlowedHandsCheck. To feel in the headset: the
      spring and the top speed (SlowSpring, SlowTopSpeed in VRHand) may want tuning.

### 351. Loose magazines collide with the held gun and with empty hands (user, 2026-10-28 evening; built 925c1d4, check in a game run)

      not grabbed". The gun's stand-in ignored magazines on purpose since 2026-10-20 (it flung them at the hand's speed). Now it
      meets them, a pushed light prop capped at the gun's speed + 1 m/s and 15 rad/s (GunProxyBody.Touch), magazines pushed out
      of things at 2 m/s at most; a magazine leaving the well still passes out until clear. Empty hands push loose magazines
      they move into faster than 0.5 m/s (HandBody.PushProps; a slow reach to take one passes through). Check: a magazine lying
      on a crate pushed by the gun and by a hand, one dropped onto the gun, a reload's ejected magazine falling clear, and that
      reaching for a magazine still takes it.

### 350. A soldier picked up is hurt by barely touching the ground (user, 2026-10-28: "When picking up a soldier they take damage even when only slightly touching the ground, maybe calculate force?")
- [ ] (Built 2026-10-28: an impact counts at the lesser of its contact speed and its impulse over a 16 kg trunk bone, BodyImpacts.ImpactMass; within 0.15 s a harder blow adds the difference. Check in a game run: lifting and dragging hurt nothing, the soldier check's slam and throw still hurt.) The held or picked-up body's impacts should hurt only by a real blow: by the force of the impact (its impulse against the
      body's mass), not by a light touch while it is lifted or dragged.
      ("troung" read as ground; if it meant the trunk, the same rule covers it: the hand's light touch on the lifted trunk is no blow.)
      Sent again on 2026-10-28 (the same words, "touching the troung", the trunk reading): still this task, nothing new added.
      Check it first in the game run: lift a soldier, put his feet on the ground, and see whether he is hurt.

  0.7): 2-6%, 22.3 ms (SteamVR's half rate, 45 fps) with the GPU at only 7 ms: the eyes' render on the main thread 3.5 ms (3.6x
  Game's), FinishFrameRendering 5.6, Update scripts 3.1, physics 1.1, the scene grown to 273 bodies (58 awake), 5158 renderers.

  BlowSpeed (the 2.2 m/s floor once), Holding() unused, the kill comment, TakeHand, Follow's player read once, test findings.
  Not changed: BashCast's up to 12 casts in a fast missed frame (the dense points are why gun swings hit at all, 2026-10-24),
  the knife's own speed setting without the 2.2 floor (it is the knife's setting), ThrowDud's cooldown (tiny), the test's
  fixed waits (the run is 60 s). Refuted by the skeptics: 9, among them a LastHit leak and a respawn kill (both handled).
- Frozen bench at the null driver's 1440x1584 per eye (GPU median, mean of two rounds; reports/shots/bench-ultra):
  Game (Fastest) 2.1 ms, Low 1.6 (old 2.2), Medium 2.8 (old 3.4), High 4.2 (old 5.7), Ultra 8.9 -> 6.0 with per-texture
  filtering (old 12.4). Ultra's terrain, reflections and soft particles: no measurable cost; High's shadows 1.1 ms, MSAA 2x 0
  (the bench's spread is about 0.5 ms between rounds: the laptop GPU warms up).
- Estimated frame rate in play on the user's PC (Quest 3 through Link, 90 Hz = 11.1 ms): anchored on the one measurement, old
  Ultra 31-46 fps in play (21.7-32 ms) against its 12.4 ms here, so play costs 1.75-2.6x the bench (more pixels, a live match).
  Estimates, not measurements; the log's once-a-minute line now says GPU and frame time for each.

| Preset | Bench GPU | Est. in play | Est. fps (cap 90) |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Low | 1.6 ms | 2.7-4.1 ms | 90 (the GPU is not the limit) |
| Medium | 2.8 ms | 4.8-7.2 ms | 90 |
| High | 4.2 ms | 7.3-10.9 ms | 90, the top of the range near the limit |
| Ultra (now) | 6.0 ms | 10.5-15.6 ms | about 64-90: may still reproject in heavy scenes |
| Ultra (before tonight) | 8.9 ms | 15.6-23 ms | about 43-64 |
| old Ultra (before 336) | 12.4 ms | 21.7-32 ms | 31-46 (measured) |

- Third workflow per-frame-garbage-hunt (5 Haiku readers given the profiler's per-section allocation figures, one skeptic
  each): fixed the gun counter's string per shot (a full-auto gun built one every 6-9 frames: each state's text made once,
  the key now exact), ScriptKeys.Find (a list and a sort twice a second for the gun in hand: a plain scan, the same key;
  the sorts no longer box), the controller type read into a new StringBuilder every 2 s (one buffer, compared in place),
  the camera's cull distances copied twice a second (a new float[32]: every 5 s), the 5 s prune list, the magazine kick's
  debug line built every 0.3 s of every reload with logging off (Log.DebugOn). Left: event-only allocations (a seat's
  wheel measure, surfacing from water, the counter on a draw, a pin search on a grenade switch), the settings panel's
  strings while it is open, the canvas scan's array once a second.

Measured at the Quest 3's size (vrshots --bench --res 2064x2208: SteamVR then asks 2528x2704 an eye, the new start line
says so), the world frozen, GPU medians (reports/shots/bench-quest). At 90 Hz the budget is 11.1 ms:

| Preset | old GPU | new GPU | fps the GPU allows (90 cap) |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Game (Fastest) | | 3.2 ms | 90 |
| Low | 3.1 ms | 2.3 ms | 90 |
| Medium | 6.8 ms | 4.9 ms | 90 |
| High | 16.6 ms | 6.9 ms | 90 (old: about 60, the ghosting) |
| Ultra | 23.7 ms | 12.2 ms | about 82 (old: about 42; the user measured 31-46 in play) |

High's shadows cost 2.8 ms here, its MSAA 2x 0.9, render scale 0.8 saves 1.4. Ultra is still 1.1 ms over 90 Hz: its
MSAA 2x and scale 1.1 variants are measured next (bench-quest2).

Correction, same night: the bench's "old High" and "old Ultra" took the new presets' texture filtering; the old ones forced
16x (cfaa7f7~1). Measured again with it (reports/shots/bench-final): Low 1.2 ms (old 2.3), Medium 2.9 (old 3.6), High 3.8
(old 8.6), Ultra 5.6 (old 11.5). So High is about 2.3x and Ultra about 2x cheaper on the GPU than before 336.

- Second workflow recent-changes-review (7 Haiku readers over the diffs of 2026-10-23/24, one skeptic each; 14 agents):
  37 findings, 29 confirmed. Fixed: the minute line's "mod's own work" (a counter the input check reset every 2 s: each
  caller has its own marks now), GunVolume's fixed rotation rebuilt every pose, ShapeFit's direction cache thrashing between
  180 and 250, an input outage overwriting the held-button snapshot (a button held through it pressed again), a belt held
  by a hand that lost tracking (now hangs from the box), the magnifier grip's script-key search every frame, the reload's
  glove trackers on an in-place switch, the intro's late title taken by the canvas manager (one in eight), the flat notice's
  string per OnGUI call, the post-process layer's GetComponent per frame, a weapon with no hand pose asked twice a frame,
  two log lines sharing one once-key, misplaced and stale comments (WeaponDrops, ShapeFit, HandModel, IntroStage,
  ReloadInteractions' summary, MagazineProp's tolerances). Not changed: the magazine copy of every renderer when nothing
  shows and no loaded look was recorded (the stand-in instead would change the look of every gun the game hides empty: needs a
  picture run), the body hiding's per-frame walk (about 20 native reads; skipping it misses a weapon swapped for one with as
  many renderers), the grenade's palm-surface sum (changes with the glove setting), the compositor read twice a frame in
  profiled runs only.
- Soldier throws, measured over 17 soldier runs (vrshots --fast --grabcheck, the throw line now names the fastest bone, when,
  alive or dead, the frames past the 25 m/s cap and the fastest bone while swung): with the motors off (0) and Unity's
  default solver, 25, 32, 51, 25, 26 m/s and no blow-up. Tried and dropped, each blowing a limb up: re-limping a body that
  died in flight plus 12/4 solver passes (2325, 1833, 1929 m/s, three of three), the death drive of 50 on held soldiers plus
  the solver passes (834, 918, 101 m/s). Kept: motors off while held and for 3 s after a throw, re-limped only while held.
  The held gun's physics stand-in no longer collides with the held soldier (it pushed and hurt him: "slammed into
  RavenfieldVR Gun Collider"). The test lifts the shield at a hand's pace (teleported a metre, the shield lost 64-127 health
  before a shot; lifted, none), sends earlier steps' soldiers away from the spot (a grab took the step before's soldier),
  gives an unarmed shooter a gun and picks another shooter when the used list comes round to the shield.
  With the kept setup (runs soldiers14-18): fastest bone after the throw 19.0, 23.7, 18.6, 33.0, 18.4 m/s and 21 while swung, no blow-up; the shield lifted with no damage in all; new step 1b (user, 2026-10-24: slamming a held soldier still hurts him): slammed down at 8 m/s while held, 100 -> 83, still held. The check now quiets every other bot (the player's own team shot the enemies brought to arm's length: shields dead before a shot, 30-40 lost before a throw). Boxing missed twice in 18 runs (the second fist after the first unbalanced him): its line now carries the fist notes.
